## 1. Universal Bounds On Entropy Production From Fluctuating Coarse-grained Trajectories

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Nat. Rev. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Nature Reviews Physics
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-07-20
- **Authors**: Udo Seifert
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s42254-026-00954-5](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42254-026-00954-5)
- **Tags**: `physics`  `entropy production`  `stochastic thermodynamics`  `coarse-graining`  `fluctuation theorems`

### TL;DR

This review presents universal lower bounds on entropy production derived from coarse-grained trajectories in stochastic thermodynamics, applicable to both steady and time-dependent regimes.

### Motivation

Entropy production is central to non-equilibrium thermodynamics but is often difficult to measure in partially observed systems, motivating the need for model-free bounds from accessible observables.

### Method

The paper reviews stochastic thermodynamics approaches that infer entropy production bounds from coarse-grained observables such as states, currents, correlations, and waiting times, without requiring full microscopic knowledge.

### Result

It highlights universal bounds that hold across various regimes and discusses progress in trajectory-level estimation, providing a unified framework for inferring dissipation from partial information.

### Conclusion

The review consolidates recent advances in bounding entropy production from coarse-grained data, offering practical tools for quantifying non-equilibrium behavior in complex systems.

*Keywords: entropy production, stochastic thermodynamics, coarse-graining, fluctuation theorems, non-equilibrium*

### Motivation

### Method

The authors introduce a repeater protocol that divides the long-distance channel into segments, each with its own entangled resource, and uses local operations and classical communication to sequentially teleport energy across the chain. They analyze the performance in terms of teleported energy and entanglement consumption.

### Result

The proposed architecture achieves a polynomial scaling of teleported energy with distance, in contrast to the exponential decay in direct QET, while maintaining a finite entanglement cost per segment.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical pathway for long-range quantum energy teleportation, with potential applications in quantum batteries and energy distribution in quantum networks.

This paper provides a complete theory of the Clifford commutant, offering explicit bases and a graphical calculus, with applications to magic measures and stabilizer property testing.

### Motivation

The Clifford group is central to quantum information, but the structure of its commutant was only partially understood for small tensor powers. A complete characterization is needed for many applications.

### Method

The authors derive an explicit orthogonal basis for the Clifford commutant for arbitrary tensor power and qubit number, introduce an alternative basis of isotropic sums of Pauli operators, and develop a graphical calculus for diagrammatic manipulation.

### Result

They compute the dimension of the commutant, show the basis is generated by permutations and at most three other operators, and apply the results to characterize measurable magic measures and identify optimal strategies for stabilizer property testing.

### Conclusion

The complete theory of the Clifford commutant enables a wealth of applications in quantum information, including magic quantification and property testing, and extends to multi-qudit systems with prime local dimension.

- **Tags**: `noise`  `readout`  `dynamical stabilizer codes`  `measurement-biased noise`  `teraquop volume`

### TL;DR

This paper optimizes measurement schedules for dynamical color codes under measurement-biased noise, introducing the teraquop volume metric and showing that belief matching decoding can significantly improve performance.

### Motivation

Dynamical stabilizer codes may enable large-scale quantum computation, but their performance depends on the measurement schedule, especially when measurements are noisy. The authors aim to optimize schedules for hardware with noisy readouts.

### Method

They analyze dynamically condensed color codes under various noise biases, using the teraquop volume (product of qubits and measurement rounds) to compare performance. They test repeating measurements and compare belief matching with minimum-weight perfect matching decoders.

### Result

Repeating measurements improves performance under measurement-biased noise but offers little benefit for unbiased or X-biased noise. The teraquop volume reveals that differences are mainly due to measurement rounds, and belief matching can turn a worst-performing code into a best-performing one.

### Conclusion

The study provides practical guidance for optimizing dynamical codes for noisy hardware, emphasizing the importance of decoding with correlated errors and using the teraquop volume for fair comparisons.

*Keywords: dynamical stabilizer codes, measurement-biased noise, teraquop volume, belief matching, quantum error correction*

### TL;DR

This paper studies the time evolution of genuine multipartite negativity in an open Kitaev quantum spin liquid coupled to a dissipative bosonic environment, revealing that non-Markovian dynamics preserves entanglement at higher temperatures.

### Motivation

Quantum spin liquids are promising for quantum computing, but their entanglement properties are usually studied in closed systems. This work addresses the realistic scenario of coupling to a dissipative environment, which is crucial for practical applications.

### Method

The authors model the Kitaev QSL coupled to a Caldeira-Leggett bosonic bath and time-evolve the system in both Markovian and non-Markovian regimes. They compute genuine multipartite negativity, quantum Fisher information, spin-spin correlators, and Wilson loop expectation values from the time-dependent density matrix.

### Result

In the Markovian regime, GMN remains nonzero only in hexagonal loopy subregions and vanishes on the same timescale as the Wilson loop expectation value. In the non-Markovian regime, GMN persists up to much higher temperatures, and emergent interactions between spins are generated.

### Conclusion

The findings show that non-Markovian dissipation can stabilize multipartite entanglement in QSLs, and engineering dissipation could tailor QSL properties, offering new avenues for quantum information processing.

This work highlights a molecular mechanism of adaptation in a pest species, with implications for pest control and evolutionary biology.
